### 4. 📋 待办
- Worker 回调到达 → 读取结果，更新 registry（完成的 eval 转 DONE + 记录 metrics），向 Director 汇报全面状态
- cuda:0 s43 eval 完成 + predictions.json 存在 → Lead 1 CED evidence filter 提交（Worker 应已做）
- cuda:1/2 eval 完成 → 收集 Ign-F1/Evi-F1，写入 report.md，向 Director 报 scaling study 结果
- cuda:3 8B training 完成 → 提交 8B eval（`--model_path rsft_output_qwen3_8b/&lt;final_ckpt> --base_model Qwen3-8B --sft_adapter sft_output_qwen3_8b_bf16`）

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
- 项目目标：EMNLP 2026 Main（ARR July），证据接地的文档级关系抽取
- 核心 story：CED（Contrastive Evidence Discrimination）作为统一信号——训练时过滤 RSFT 数据 + 推理时过滤 distractor citations
- **Lead 1**（inference-time CED filtering）是最高优先级——如果 EDCR 从 0.63→0.80+ 就是强 claim
- **Lead 2**（RSFT→GRPO two-stage）step 30/50 时 reward 0.96→1.02 趋势好，但 checkpoint 已删（磁盘清理），需要重跑
- Scaling study（1.7B/4B/8B + LLaMA cross-arch）是支撑 story 的配角实验
- Evidence Tax 现象：1.7B +6.2pp, 4B -7.5pp, 8B ≈0——CED-RSFT 应该消除这个 tax
- 易错点：eval 命令必须带 `--sft_adapter`（漏掉 → rel_f1≈0）；GRPO 用 `--data_dir` 不是 `--data_path`；`--no-quantize` 对 5090 GPU 是必须的（bf16 原生比量化快）

## 项目硬约束 (memory.md 节选)

## GPU 需求

- CED scoring 使用 DeBERTa-v3-base NLI 模型，~400MB 显存，**任意一张卡即可**
- 3 个 scoring 作业可在不同 GPU 上并行跑，不影响进行中的 generation
- 计算量主要在 CPU 侧

    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

Now I have the SFT baselines. Let me compile the comparison:

**SFT Baselines (evidence-SFT):**
- Qwen3-4B: Ign-F1=0.3974, F1=0.4049, Evi-F1=0.7986, EDCR=0.6982
- Qwen3-1.7B: Ign-F1=0.4786, F1=0.4945, Evi-F1=0.8316, EDCR=0.6704


- Qwen3-8B: Ign-F1=0.5051, F1=0.5262, Evi-F1=0.8188, EDCR=0.651
- LLaMA-3.1-8B: Ign-F1=0.5384, F1=0.5514, Evi-F1=0.8151, EDCR=0.628

Now looking at the RSFT results to see how the new approach compares, starting with Qwen3-4B which shows some improvement in the ignorance F1 metric. Looking at the evidence faithfulness scores, the gains are more modest—Qwen3-4B and LLaMA show small improvements around 0.77-1.04 percentage points, while Qwen3-1.7B essentially stays flat. What's more interesting is the EDCR metric, where all three models show meaningful drops of 4+ percentage points after RSFT, which indicates the fine-tuning is actually making them more faithful to their evidence rather than hallucinating. Looking at the s43 evaluation result, it's nearly identical to s44 at 0.4511, which confirms consistency. The RSFT approach is interesting—it recovers 5.38 percentage points from the evidence penalty, bringing the 4B model from 0.3974 up to 0.4512, though it still trails the no-evidence baseline of 0.4721 by about 2 percentage points.

Let me verify the math on the evidence tax: the original penalty was 7.47 percentage points, and RSFT recovers 5.38 of that, which amounts to a 72% recovery rate. That's a remarkably strong result for CED-RSFT.

Now looking at the 1.7B model, the pattern flips entirely—evidence actually helps here, giving a negative tax of -8.04pp, and RSFT pushes even further with an additional 2.64pp gain. For LLaMA-8B, I'm seeing a much smaller evidence tax of around 0.68pp, which suggests the effect varies significantly across model scales.
